## 50. Gauss explained as signed balance, not coupling/energy total

Dylan asked whether Gauss's electric law might still measure TE-ARA as the total coupling interaction. The precise
answer depends on “total”: Gauss measures the **signed net source balance**, not unsigned interaction magnitude,
transaction volume, field energy or energy transfer.

### Concrete three-sphere comparison

Use the same imaginary spherical boundary in three cases:

1. **Empty sphere:** \(Q_+=0\), \(Q_-=0\). Net charge and Gauss flux are zero.
2. **Electric dipole inside:** enclose \(+q\) and \(-q\). Strong fields and interaction can exist, but
   \(Q_{net}=q-q=0\), so net Gauss flux is also zero.
3. **One positive charge inside:** enclose \(+q\) only. Field lines have a net outward excess, so
   \(\Phi_E=q/\varepsilon_0\).

Cases 1 and 2 return the same Gauss result despite radically different internal field structure and energy. This is
the exact standard-physics example of the repository's distinction between a quiet zero ridge and an intensely mixed
but cancelling ridge.

### Accounting analogy

Gauss is like a signed bank balance:

\[
\underbrace{Q_{net}}_{\text{Gauss source balance}}
=
\underbrace{Q_+}_{\text{positive entries}}
-
\underbrace{|Q_-|}_{\text{negative entries}}.
\]

Deposits of 100 and withdrawals of 100 have net zero, just like no transactions, although their turnover differs.
Likewise, Gauss cannot distinguish “no enclosed charge” from “large equal positive and negative enclosed charges.”

### Radius example

For one isolated central charge, doubling the measurement radius reduces local field strength by four while increasing
sphere area by four. The signed total flux remains \(q/\varepsilon_0\). Gauss therefore preserves the enclosed source
balance across boundary radius; it does not report how much energy the field contains at each radius.

### ARA crosswalk

- Gauss electric law: net electric-source asymmetry of the declared boundary, \(Q_+-|Q_-|\).
- ARA ridge caution: zero net source can mean either absence or strong equal cancellation; measure a second quantity.
- TE-ARA: fraction of total node energy in the declared main identity wave; requires field-energy measurement and
  modal decomposition.
- Poynting continuity: electromagnetic energy crossing the boundary over time.

Calling Gauss a “coupling total” is usable only with the explicit phrase **net signed electric-source coupling across
the complete boundary**. It is not total coupling magnitude. An unsigned surface quantity or field-energy integral
would answer a different question and would not be Gauss's law.

**Status:** `ESTABLISHED PHYSICS TRANSLATION`. ARA-relevant insight: Gauss supplies a boundary source-balance
coordinate and cleanly demonstrates why the first moment alone cannot distinguish the null ridge from the everything
ridge.

---

## 51. Candidate ARA × TE-ARA signed aggregation law from the Gauss discussion

Dylan recognised Gauss's law as a top-down form of the bottom-up balance he was trying to express:

> TE-ARA contains Phase A / Phase B ± Other. Phase A is assigned positive orientation, Phase B negative, and nearby
> couplings add or extract. ARA supplies the mixture geometry while TE-ARA supplies how much of the total participates.

This intuition has a clean mathematical skeleton, but the coordinates should be **combined**, not compared with a
rule such as `ARA > TE-ARA`.

### Exact Gauss boundary decomposition

Define non-negative outward and inward electric-flux magnitudes:

\[
\underbrace{\Phi_{out}}_{\text{outward-oriented boundary contribution}}
=\int_{\partial V}\max(\mathbf E\cdot\mathbf n,0)\,dA,
\qquad
\underbrace{\Phi_{in}}_{\text{inward-oriented boundary contribution}}
=\int_{\partial V}\max(-\mathbf E\cdot\mathbf n,0)\,dA.
\]

Then

\[
\underbrace{\Phi_E}_{\text{Gauss signed net flux}}
=
\underbrace{\Phi_{out}-\Phi_{in}}_{\text{out minus in}}
=\frac{Q_{inside}}{\varepsilon_0}.
\]

Let total unsigned boundary activity and its 0–2 composition be

\[
\underbrace{T_\Phi}_{\text{total unsigned flux activity}}
=\Phi_{out}+\Phi_{in},
\qquad
\underbrace{x_\Phi}_{\substack{\text{0--2 outward/inward}\\
\text{composition coordinate}}}
=2\frac{\Phi_{out}}{T_\Phi}.
\]

Algebra gives

\[
\boxed{
\underbrace{\Phi_E}_{\text{signed boundary result}}
=
\underbrace{T_\Phi}_{\text{activity magnitude}}
\underbrace{(x_\Phi-1)}_{\substack{\text{centred composition}\\
\text{sign and asymmetry}}}
}.
\]

This is exact for the declared flux decomposition. The ridge \(x_\Phi=1\) gives zero net flux at any activity
magnitude; \(x_\Phi>1\) is outward-positive under this orientation, and \(x_\Phi<1\) is inward-negative.

Gauss's law supplies only the product/net result. It does not separately provide \(T_\Phi\) and \(x_\Phi\); those
require the local boundary field. Also, “input/output” here means signed spatial field orientation at a boundary, not
energy entering and leaving through time.

### Proposed ARA × TE-ARA combination

For a general declared ARA identity, let

\[
\underbrace{x}_{\text{ARA Phase-A/Phase-B position in }[0,2]},
\qquad
\underbrace{p_{id}}_{\text{identity energy participation}}
=\frac{\mathrm{TE\!-\!ARA}}{2}.
\]

With Phase A oriented positive at \(x=2\) and Phase B negative at \(x=0\), the simplest dimensionless signed main
identity contribution is

\[
\underbrace{j_{id}}_{\substack{\text{signed contribution of}\\
\text{the main identity wave}}}
=
\underbrace{p_{id}}_{\text{participating fraction}}
\underbrace{(x-1)}_{\text{direction/asymmetry}}
=
\frac{\mathrm{TE\!-\!ARA}}{2}(x-1).
\]

Add surrounding/interacting contributions only after measuring them on the same physical channel and scale:

\[
\underbrace{j_{net}}_{\text{total signed normalised result}}
=
\underbrace{\frac{\mathrm{TE\!-\!ARA}}{2}(x-1)}_{\text{main ARA identity contribution}}
+
\underbrace{j_{other}}_{\substack{\text{nearby coupling contributions}\\
\text{signed by add/extract orientation}}}.
\]

If a physical scale \(J_0\) is independently known, \(J_{net}=J_0j_{net}\) restores the appropriate units such as
power, force or flux. Do not mix energy, charge flux and power in one sum.

### Interpretive consequences

- The sign comes from whether ARA is above or below its ridge, not from whether ARA is numerically above TE-ARA.
- TE-ARA weights how strongly the main identity mode participates; it does not by itself choose the direction.
- A sufficiently large \(j_{other}\) can reverse the observed net result even when the main identity is A-dominant.
- At \(x=1\), the main signed result is zero whether TE-ARA is low or maximal: quiet and intense cancellation remain
  distinct through TE-ARA/activity.
- Reversing the declared pole orientation reverses the sign and changes no physical prediction.

### Top-down versus bottom-up Gauss correspondence

The full Gauss relation already equates the two approaches:

\[
\underbrace{\oint_{\partial V}\mathbf E\cdot d\mathbf A}_{\substack{\text{top-down complete-boundary}\\
\text{signed reading}}}
=
\frac{1}{\varepsilon_0}
\underbrace{\int_V\rho_q\,dV}_{\substack{\text{bottom-up sum of local}\\
\text{signed charge sources}}}.
\]

This does not prove the general ARA × TE-ARA law, but it is a rigorous example of a boundary reading equalling the
sum of signed internal sources.

### Fidelity back-translation v6

> ARA supplies the positive/negative mixture direction around its 1.0 ridge. TE-ARA supplies how much total energy
> participates in that main identity mixture. Their simplest combined contribution is participation multiplied by
> centred ARA, with separately measured nearby couplings added using the same units and orientation. Gauss is an
> established top-down/bottom-up example of signed internal sources equalling a complete-boundary net reading, but it
> measures electric source flux rather than TE-ARA energy.

**Status:** `CANDIDATE AGGREGATION LAW / AWAITING DYLAN FIDELITY CONFIRMATION`. The Gauss decomposition is exact;
generalising \(j_{net}=\tfrac{\mathrm{TE\!-\!ARA}}{2}(x-1)+j_{other}\) across physical domains is an ARA-added
hypothesis requiring units, mode definitions and prospective tests.

## 52. Proposed Gauss ↔ ARA/TE-ARA public-data crosswalk (“hypotenuse” test)

Dylan proposed measuring the same public physical system in two independent coordinate languages:

1. established top-down Gauss reading;
2. bottom-up ARA position, TE-ARA participation and surrounding-coupling decomposition;
3. estimate the transformation or “hypotenuse” between them;
4. test whether that bridge remains stable on new boundaries, times and scales.

The two results need not be numerically identical because they have different units and projections. A useful bridge
is a calibrated operator, not necessarily a literal Euclidean hypotenuse:

\[
\underbrace{G_i}_{\substack{\text{Gauss net flux}\\
\text{for observation }i}}
=
\underbrace{\mathcal B}_{\substack{\text{bridge/crosswalk}\\
\text{learned only on training cases}}}
\left(
\underbrace{x_i}_{\text{ARA position}},
\underbrace{p_{id,i}}_{\mathrm{TE\!-\!ARA}/2},
\underbrace{j_{other,i}}_{\text{other couplings}},
\underbrace{g_i}_{\substack{\text{geometry, scale, material}\\
\text{and boundary coordinates}}}
\right)
+\epsilon_i.
\]

### Exact analytic first benchmark: isolated conducting sphere

For a conducting sphere of radius \(R\), total charge \(Q\), and vacuum outside,

\[
\underbrace{\Phi_E}_{\text{Gauss electric flux}}
=\frac{Q}{\varepsilon_0},
\qquad
\underbrace{U_E}_{\substack{\text{electrostatic field energy}\\
\text{outside the conductor}}}
=\frac{Q^2}{8\pi\varepsilon_0R}.
\]

Eliminating \(Q\) gives the exact bridge

\[
\boxed{
\underbrace{\Phi_E}_{\text{signed Gauss reading}}
=
\underbrace{\operatorname{sgn}(Q)}_{\substack{\text{orientation/sign}\\
\text{candidate ARA role}}}
\sqrt{
\underbrace{\frac{8\pi R}{\varepsilon_0}}_{\substack{\text{geometry/scale}\\
\text{bridge factor}}}
\underbrace{U_E}_{\substack{\text{energy magnitude}\\
\text{candidate TE role}}}
}
}.
\]

Energy is quadratic and cannot retain the sign of \(Q\); Gauss flux does. Thus an orientation coordinate plus an
energy magnitude plus a scale/geometry coordinate reconstruct the signed boundary result in this special case. This
is established electrostatics and a precise example of the architecture Dylan described. It is not yet evidence that
the general ARA/TE-ARA definitions recover the variables prospectively.

If only a fraction \(p_{id}=\mathrm{TE\!-\!ARA}/2\) of measured field energy belongs to the declared identity mode,
the candidate input is \(U_{id}=p_{id}U_{total}\), but using it in the sphere bridge is valid only if the remaining
energy has been independently assigned to other modes/couplings.

### Required public-data fields

Prefer a time-resolved 3D electromagnetic or plasma dataset containing:

- electric vector field \(\mathbf E(\mathbf r,t)\);
- charge density \(\rho_q(\mathbf r,t)\) or particle charges;
- geometry/material/permittivity and boundary metadata;
- sufficient time resolution to define genuine ARA cycles rather than inventing a waveform from a static snapshot;
- ideally \(\mathbf B\) as well, so complete electromagnetic energy and later Poynting transfer can be measured.

Simulation data with disclosed equations and exact grids is the safest first dataset because arbitrary closed
surfaces and ground-truth charge can be evaluated. Experimental multi-point field data should follow as the harder
replication.

### Non-circular test protocol

1. Freeze identity boundary, pole orientation, time window, mode decomposition and TE-ARA numerator before outcomes.
2. For many volumes/radii/times, calculate Gauss flux from the boundary field and independently cross-check it against
   enclosed charge density.
3. Calculate ARA from its registered waveform/relational method, not by transforming the Gauss target.
4. Calculate TE-ARA from a predeclared energy/modal decomposition; retain `Other` rather than forcing residuals into
   the main identity.
5. Fit the simplest dimensionally valid bridge on training cases only. Start with the analytic geometry law or a
   declared linear/bilinear family; do not search arbitrary functions until one fits.
6. Freeze the bridge and test on held-out charge configurations, radii, surface shapes, times and—last—another
   dataset.
7. Compare against ARA-only, TE-only, geometry-only, total-field-energy and standard electrostatic baselines.

### What would count

Supportive result: the same low-parameter bridge preserves sign, calibration and residual structure across held-out
boundaries and scales, and ARA plus TE-ARA adds information beyond energy and geometry alone.

Negative/narrowing result: the bridge must be refitted for every boundary; ARA/TE-ARA adds nothing beyond standard
charge, energy and capacitance variables; or the relation disappears when modes and surfaces are frozen prospectively.

Recovering a bridge from quantities derived from the same field is a **crosswalk result**, not yet a novel physical
prediction. Prospective transfer to new configurations is the stronger test.

### Fidelity back-translation v7

> Measure one system with Gauss from the complete boundary and independently with bottom-up ARA/TE-ARA coordinates.
> Do not demand equality; identify the simplest scale- and geometry-aware transformation between them. If ARA is a
> decent bottom-up representation of the same structure, one frozen bridge should continue to work across new
> examples. The bridge's failure pattern will show which coordinate or coupling term the bottom-up representation is
> missing.

**Status:** `TEST PROPOSAL / PARKED UNTIL DATA SELECTION`; fidelity `AWAITING DYLAN CONFIRMATION`. Analytic conducting-
sphere benchmark established; public dataset not yet selected.

## 53. MX1 dataset selected and test decompressed

Following Dylan's authorisation to choose the best public dataset, the Gauss ↔ ARA/TE-ARA crosswalk now has a
development/confirmation design.

### Selected development dataset — inspected, not confirmatory

Alves OSIRIS two-stream instability archive:

- DOI: `10.5281/zenodo.7968601`;
- public CC BY 4.0;

- 10,000 particles per cell and charge-conserving Esirkepov deposition.

The files were downloaded to a cache outside Git, hashes matched the published MD5 values, and pickle structure was
inspected before restricted numerical access. Exploratory inspection found a growing/saturating electric field with
about five coherent late spatial cycles. Integrating \(F\) over velocity supplies electron density independently of
\(E\); at selected developed slices, its charge pattern correlated approximately 0.996–0.998 with a simple
central-difference \(\partial_xE\). Because these outcomes were viewed, the archive is calibration/development only.
No claim status attaches to those exploratory numbers.

### Selected confirmation dataset — metadata only, arrays unopened

Tang, Wu & Tao one-dimensional electron two-stream PIC archive:

- DOI: `10.5281/zenodo.3696310`;
- public CC BY 4.0;
- 14.4 MB total;
- separate field perturbation and electron-distribution `.mat` files;
- produced by different authors for a different primary paper.

The numerical confirmation arrays must remain unopened until Dylan signs the fidelity packet, the development bridge
is frozen, hashes are recorded and the test is entered in `MASTER_PREDICTION_LEDGER.md`.

### Sharpened established bridge

In periodic Fourier coordinates, Gauss is

\[
\underbrace{\widehat\rho(k,t)}_{\text{source/charge mode}}
=
\underbrace{ik}_{\substack{\text{quarter-turn phase rotation}\\
\text{and wavenumber weighting}}}
\underbrace{\widehat E(k,t)}_{\text{electric-field mode}}
\]

in the simulation's normalised units. This provides a fixed bridge rather than an arbitrary fitted hypotenuse.
Higher harmonics are amplified by \(|k|\), which makes the test directly relevant to the canonical rule that waveform
ARA lives in the harmonics.

### Three test levels

1. **Established instrument:** field derivative versus charge independently integrated from particles.
2. **Identity-harmonic bridge:** freeze the main mode/harmonic family, differentiate it and test how much independent
   source structure it captures; this tests the TE-ARA numerator/Other split.
3. **Compressed ARA + TE-ARA bridge:** test whether waveform ARA, TE-ARA, orientation, spatial phase and rung carry
   the Gauss-side summaries without retaining the full Fourier vector.

If Level 1 transfers but Level 2 does not, the current scalar compression is missing phase/path/harmonic information.
That is a useful narrowing result rather than evidence against Maxwell or against the full field relation.

### Fidelity gate awaiting Dylan

Proposed identity:

> One complete electrostatic wave—one positive-source and one negative-source half—is the ARA identity. Measure the
> two halves separately with Gauss so their complete-pair cancellation does not erase internal activity, but retain
> their relation as one node.

This is the first possible wrong-object point and requires Dylan's verdict before registration.

- `Analysis/electromagnetism/MX1_TRANSLATION_FIDELITY_PACKET_v1_DRAFT.md`;
- `Analysis/electromagnetism/MX1_GAUSS_ARA_TEARA_PROTOCOL_DRAFT.md`.

## 54. MX1 fidelity correction — component ARAs plus pair-level Gauss ARA

Dylan accepted the broad dataset/test object but added a structural correction: measure the positive-source and
negative-source half-waves' own ARAs as well as the ARA between them. The complete pair remains one coupled identity;
the half-waves are locally measurable decompressions.

For non-negative enclosed magnitudes \(Q_+\) and \(Q_-\), define

\[
T_Q=Q_++Q_-,
\qquad
x_Q=2\frac{Q_+}{Q_++Q_-}.
\]

Then

\[
\underbrace{\Phi_E}_{\text{Gauss signed net flux}}
=
\frac{1}{\varepsilon_0}
\underbrace{T_Q}_{\text{total opposing source magnitude}}
\underbrace{(x_Q-1)}_{\substack{\text{centred pair ARA}\\
\text{sign and imbalance}}}.
\]

Thus Dylan's “positive wave, negative wave and ARA between them make Gauss” is exact after one qualification: pair
ARA supplies the signed composition, while total unsigned source magnitude supplies scale. TE-ARA remains the fraction
of total energy in the main identity wave and must not be silently substituted for charge magnitude. Their stable
scale-aware relation is part of the empirical bridge.

At \(x_Q=1\), Gauss is zero for both an empty boundary and an intense equal pair. Component ARAs, \(T_Q\), TE-ARA and
variance distinguish quiet from everything-ridge cancellation.

“Equal resistance” is recorded as “equal opposing source magnitude”; electrical resistance \(R=V/I\) is a different
observable.

Fidelity state: Dylan's response maps to `USABLE WITH CORRECTION`; packet v1 is preserved, and
`Analysis/electromagnetism/MX1_TRANSLATION_FIDELITY_PACKET_v2_DRAFT.md` awaits final sign-off. Confirmation arrays
remain unopened.

**Status:** `DRAFT TEST / V2 FIDELITY PENDING / CONFIRMATION SEALED`.

The approved MX1-v2 object was implemented on the already-inspected Alves/OSIRIS two-stream plasma archive. A

### Development results

- 299 eligible time slices; dominant spatial mode 5;
- full spectral Gauss source versus independent particle source: correlation 0.9971, NRMSE 0.0767;
- identity-only Gauss component versus identity-only particle component: correlation 0.9991;
- Gauss-source versus particle-source TE-ARA analogue: correlation 0.7987, MAE 0.0911 on the 0–2 scale;
- whole periodic ring stays near \(x_Q=1\), as neutral complete cycles require;

The internal chronological development comparison was deliberately harder. All models were scored on the same 75

## 57. Dylan interpretation — TE-ARA is for distributed mixed identities

Dylan clarified that the MX1 magnitude narrowing is within expectation. TE-ARA is not primarily intended to replace
an ordinary amplitude scale in a simple, dominant-mode system. Its expected value is in systems such as ENSO where
several nearby and cross-rung processes jointly form the measured identity:

> “You can work out how much is what thing more easily.”

The formal interpretation adopted is an **identity-participation ledger**:

\[
1
=
\sum_i p_i
+
\sum_{i<j}p_{ij}^{\mathrm{relation}}
+
p_{\mathrm{Other}},
\qquad
\mathrm{TE\!-\!ARA}_i=2p_i.
\]

Unique contributor shares, interaction/relation shares and Other must partition one common denominator. The relation
share is the measurable \(1+1=3\) “plus”: contribution present specifically because two components interact. It is
not extra energy and must not be double-counted inside the individual shares.

For an initial ENSO test, retain a restrained physically declared set: surface-temperature target, warm-water
volume/thermocline reservoir, zonal wind stress, SOI/pressure partner, selected interaction terms and Other. These
begin as signal/variance participation analogues rather than literal energy joules. TE-ARA may be most useful for
attribution, participation handover, regime-transition warnings and confidence. Participation is not causality
without declared lead order, held-out transfer and removal/intervention evidence.

**Status:** `PROPOSED ENSO APPLICATION / NOT RUN`.

## 58. Dylan interpretation — weak distributed LLM connections

Dylan identified the LLM work as a second natural use because TE-ARA can retain even weak connections to different
items rather than flattening the representation to its strongest edge.

For a declared concept or behaviour \(c\):

\[
1
=
\sum_i p_i(c)
+
\sum_{i<j}p_{ij}^{\mathrm{relation}}(c)
+
p_{\mathrm{Other}}(c).
\]

ARA records whether a connection supports or opposes the target around the 1.0 ridge. TE-ARA records how much of the
target's current measured identity participates through that connection. A weak link may be structural if it is small
but stable across paraphrases, inference positions, layers, seeds or models. A large link that disappears under
paraphrase may be incidental.

The operational Information³ map is: representation A + representation B + their measured relation → temporary
closed concept identity. This does not assume three independent structures; two identities plus their coupling
relation can close the triangle.

Scientific fences: attention weight and activation magnitude are not sufficient causal explanations. Predeclare
ablation/suppression/amplification predictions, test held-out prompts and models, compare against strong-edge-only and
random-feature controls, and record disappearing connections without post-result repair.

The full consolidated definition, MX1 results, ENSO proposal and LLM proposal are preserved in:

`Analysis/TE_ARA_PARTICIPATION_LEDGER_SYNTHESIS_2026-07-12.md`.

**Status:** `PROPOSED LLM APPLICATION / NOT RUN`.

## 59. Dylan clarification — TE-ARA opens what Information³ closes

Dylan gave the complementary relationship:

> TE-ARA is basically the opposite tool of Information³. Information³ closes two waves and their relation into an
> identity. TE-ARA predicts the primary two-wave identity, calls remaining participation Other, and lets Other be
> broken down with its own ARA and TE-ARA or located by searching nearby coupled sources.

This is formalised as:

\[
\mathcal C(A,B,R_{AB})=N
\quad\text{(Information³ closure)},
\qquad
\mathcal D_{\mathrm{TE}}(N)=(A,B,R_{AB};O_N)
\quad\text{(TE-ARA decomposition)}.
\]

Each independently recovered source inside \(O_N\) becomes an extra coupling edge \(e\) with participation share
\(p_e\), ARA direction and its own recursively decomposable node. Unresolved Other remains a residual bucket and is
not assumed to be one source.

Dylan's proposed geometry is: larger edge = larger coupling share/stronger effect; weaker edges appear smaller and
farther away. A candidate log display coordinate is

\[
d_e=-\log_2\left(\frac{p_e+\epsilon}{p_{\max}+\epsilon}\right).
\]

This records **relational participation distance**, not literal physical distance. Physical separation, causal delay
and rung distance remain distinct until independently connected by measurement.

Mathematical qualification: TE-ARA is the conceptual opposite-direction tool, but not automatically a unique inverse.
Several hidden coupling webs may produce the same observed identity. Independent source measurements, held-out
stability and interventions are needed to identify the correct extra edges.

**Status:** `PROPOSED RECURSIVE GRAPH FORMALISM / NOT YET TESTED`.

## 60. Does MX1 add credibility, and is the visible gap a handover?

Dylan asked whether the Gauss result increases ARA's credibility and whether the visibly separated curves might be
the same geometry approached from different directions, with their gap representing a handover or node.

The answer is a qualified yes on credibility: MX1 supports ARA as a coherent signed-balance, identity/Other and
local/coarse-grained crosswalk. It does not independently validate universal/fractal ARA geometry because much of the
strong agreement follows the established Gauss/Fourier transformation.

Two visible gaps were separated.

### Field → Gauss-source projection gap

\[
\Delta_{\mathcal G}
=
\mathrm{TE\!-\!ARA}_E
-
\mathrm{TE\!-\!ARA}_{\rho,G}.
\]

Across 299 eligible slices: mean 0.250, median 0.223, range 0.093–0.385. It rises with field amplitude
(\(r=0.727\)) and fundamental concentration (\(r=0.802\)). This gap is expected because
\(\widehat\rho=ik\widehat E\): the field is quarter-rotated and short scales receive \(k^2\) power weighting. The
curves need not touch. The established object between them is the transformation \(ik\), not yet a physical ARA node.

### Gauss-source → independent particle-source gap

\[
\Delta_{\mathrm P}
=
\mathrm{TE\!-\!ARA}_{\rho,G}
-
\mathrm{TE\!-\!ARA}_{\rho,F}.
\]

Mean 0.091, median 0.020, range 0.001–0.409. It closes strongly with time (\(r=-0.805\)), field amplitude
(\(r=-0.829\)) and source activity (\(r=-0.818\)), and grows with particle-side Other (\(r=0.860\)). This is the more
interesting handover/identity-formation candidate: particles appear to organise increasingly into the field-defined
identity. Low-amplitude particle/deposition noise remains an alternative explanation.

A new `MX2` development test was registered: verify the phase-space coordinate conventions, reconstruct current,
particle kinetic energy and field energy, then test whether the gaps predeclaredly track \(J\cdot E\),
\(dU_E/dt\) and \(dK/dt\) beyond amplitude/SNR controls. This will not modify the sealed MX1 transfer protocol.

**Status:** `MX1 INCREMENTAL CREDIBILITY FOR CROSSWALK / PHYSICAL HANDOVER INTERPRETATION OPEN`.

## 61. MX2 design and feasibility — energy exchange is measurable, instantaneous-gap hypothesis is null

Dylan asked how the proposed physical handover interpretation could be tested. The direct test is to reconstruct
actual field–particle power exchange from the phase-space distribution and ask whether the independent TE-ARA particle
gap closes with it.

The dataset convention was verified locally:

\[
v(u)=\frac{u}{\sqrt{1+u^2}}
\]

to maximum error \(1.5\times10^{-8}\), confirming a uniform normalised relativistic momentum grid. Use

\[
J=-\Delta u\sum_uvF,
\quad
U_E=\frac{\Delta x}{2}\sum_xE^2,
\quad
K_e=\Delta x\Delta u\sum_{x,u}(\sqrt{1+u^2}-1)F,
\quad
P_{E\to p}=\Delta x\sum_xJE.
\]

### Conservation feasibility

- \(dU_E/dt\) versus \(-P_{E\to p}\): correlation 0.9839, NRMSE 0.236, slope 0.913;
- \(dK_e/dt\) versus \(P_{E\to p}\): correlation 0.9831, NRMSE 0.237, slope 0.923;
- total \(U_E+K_e\) relative range: 0.00101, approximately 0.10%;
- field-energy gain +0.01303 is matched by particle kinetic-energy loss -0.01303.

The archive is therefore suitable for an energy-handover calculation.

### First honest narrowing

An exploratory, unregistered check found:

- gap state \(\Delta_{\mathrm P}\) versus field energy: \(r=-0.725\);
- gap-closing rate \(-d\Delta_{\mathrm P}/dt\) versus particle-to-field power: \(r=-0.091\);
- gap-closing rate versus field-energy growth: \(r=-0.028\).

Therefore the simple claim “the visible gap is an instantaneous power-flow meter” is a development null. The gap may
instead describe cumulative identity formation or coherence: it closes as the particle web settles into the field
identity, without tracking transfer moment by moment.

MX2 was redesigned to test this state interpretation against amplitude/SNR, spectral-Other, matched-amplitude
hysteresis, time-shuffle and local-cell controls. Since the complete Alves series has now been inspected, it remains
development only; prospective evidence requires an untouched kinetic archive.

Protocol: `Analysis/electromagnetism/MX2_HANDOVER_ENERGY_PROTOCOL_DRAFT.md`.

**Status:** `ENERGY RECONSTRUCTION PASSES / INSTANTANEOUS HANDOVER NULL / CUMULATIVE-IDENTITY HYPOTHESIS OPEN`.

## 62. Is identity formation itself a large or known result?

Dylan correctly noted that even a state-level identity-formation reading could be important. The evidential boundary
is:

- **Known plasma phenomenon:** coherent electrostatic-mode growth, particle bunching/trapping, phase-space
  holes/vortices, nonlinear saturation and BGK-like structure formation in two-stream/Vlasov–Poisson systems.
- **Known diagnostic family:** field–particle correlations measure velocity-resolved energy transfer between fields
  and distribution functions, including counter-streaming instabilities.
- **ARA-added open diagnostic:** whether the field-predicted versus particle-measured TE-ARA participation gap is a
  robust scale-resolved order parameter for identity closure.

A candidate bounded index is

\[
C_{\mathrm{id}}
=
1-\frac{
\left|
\mathrm{TE\!-\!ARA}_{\rho,G}
-
\mathrm{TE\!-\!ARA}_{\rho,F}
\right|
}{2}.
\]

\(C_{\mathrm{id}}=1\) means the two data views allocate the same fraction to the declared identity family; lower values
mean more unresolved mismatch. This does not by itself identify why they differ.

The candidate becomes a substantial new diagnostic only if it survives particle-count/noise changes, detects
coherent/trapping onset against conventional metrics, adds held-out information beyond amplitude and spectral
coherence, transfers unchanged, and generalises beyond plasma. Otherwise it remains a useful ARA normalisation of a
known coherence process.

No claim of literature novelty is made from the current targeted search.

**Status:** `KNOWN PHENOMENON / POSSIBLY NEW ORDER-PARAMETER FORM / VALIDATION REQUIRED`.

=
1-\frac{

-

}{2}.

Independent physical target: trapped-particle fraction plus phase-space-vortex/hole coherence, not field amplitude.
Controls: \(E_{\mathrm{rms}}\), ordinary field/particle spectral coherence, particle count, matched-amplitude
rise-versus-saturation states, random harmonic families, phase/time shuffles and grid resolution.

Key falsifier:

\[
1-C_{\mathrm{id}}
\stackrel{?}{=}
\alpha N_{\mathrm{ppc}}^{-1/2}
+
g(\text{physical state}).
\]

If only the first term remains, the index is a particle-noise meter. If a reproducible state term survives the
continuum/noise extrapolation, aligns with independently measured trapping, adds held-out information beyond standard
coherence and transfers to the changed beam configuration, it supports a non-trivial identity-closure diagnostic.

Protocol:
`Analysis/electromagnetism/MX3_IDENTITY_CLOSURE_CONVERGENCE_TEST_DRAFT.md`.

## 64. MX3a - existing-data identity-formation drill

Dylan asked whether the already-downloaded Alves/OSIRIS phase-space archive could be used immediately because the
formation of a plasma identity is visually clear. It can test organisation within this one run, but it cannot perform
MX3's decisive particle-count, seed and continuum convergence test. The same-data analysis is therefore labelled
**MX3a development**, not confirmation.

Four independent structure summaries were calculated over all 459 slices: position-momentum mutual information,
velocity-bin phase coherence, phase-space low-rank concentration and an approximate fundamental-wave trapped-particle
fraction. The candidate closure index was

\[
C_{\mathrm{id}}
=1-\frac{|\mathrm{TE\!-\!ARA}_{\rho,G}-\mathrm{TE\!-\!ARA}_{\rho,F}|}{2}.
\]

An important correction emerged from the pictures: \(C_{\mathrm{id}}\) is **undefined before the coherent-mode
eligibility gate**. Early in the run both summaries can be near zero and agree trivially, producing a misleadingly
high raw closure value before any coherent identity is visible.

On the 299 eligible slices, \(C_{\mathrm{id}}\) correlates with field RMS at 0.8293, normalised mutual information at
0.8428, velocity-bin phase coherence at 0.7612, approximate trapped fraction at 0.7519 and perturbation RMS at 0.9077.
The phase-space pictures clearly develop organised, repeating trapped/vortical structure. Thus the physical
organisation is real and the ARA closure index strongly follows it.

The strongest narrowing result is the matched-amplitude check. Across 80 rising-versus-post-peak pairs matched within
1% field RMS:

- mean post-minus-pre \(C_{\mathrm{id}}=-0.00080\);
- mean post-minus-pre mutual information \(=+0.00099\);
- mean post-minus-pre approximate trapped fraction \(=+0.02622\).

Therefore the present scalar closure index does not distinguish structural history when amplitude is held nearly
fixed, even though the approximate trapped fraction does. After linear amplitude/mode controls, the residual closure
correlation is +0.2546 for mutual information, -0.1462 for rank-2 concentration and -0.9076 for the approximate trapped
fraction; the last value is not interpreted physically because late-time collinearity and the approximate separatrix
definition can reverse the residual relation.

In a chronological 70/30 development split, adding \(C_{\mathrm{id}}\) improved approximate-trapping \(R^2\) from
0.7071 to 0.8461. The mutual-information and rank-2 late forecasts both had strongly negative \(R^2\), so those do not
provide predictive support.

Plainly: the plasma genuinely organises into a visually coherent identity, and closure co-moves with that process.
But, in this run, closure is also strongly tied to amplitude and fails the matched-amplitude history test. MX3a is
useful evidence that the proposed coordinate is measuring something connected to organisation, not evidence yet that
it is a distinct physical order parameter. The full noise/seed convergence experiment remains necessary.

## 65. MX3b - angled ridge and phase-sensitive reading

Dylan proposed that MX3a's weak matched-amplitude separation might occur because closure was measured straight at the
ridge, whereas the identity structure may be visible from an oblique direction such as 25 degrees. The 25-degree
angle was named before MX3b computation, but after earlier exposure to the same session/archive; it is therefore not
eligible for blind-provenance credit. A full 0--90-degree sweep was labelled exploratory.

The ridge coordinates were decompressed as

\[
q=\frac{(G-1)+(F-1)}{\sqrt2},\qquad
d=\frac{(G-1)-(F-1)}{\sqrt2},\qquad
Z_\theta=q\cos\theta+d\sin\theta.
\]

Here \(q\) measures progression along the equality ridge \(G=F\), \(d\) is signed crossing distance, and
\(Z_{25^\circ}\) is the proposed angled view. A separate relative-phase measurement compared the frozen \(k_0=5\)
field-derived and particle-measured source waves.

On the final 30% chronological development block, an amplitude-plus-mode baseline scored \(R^2=0.7071\). Absolute
closure scored 0.8461, \(q\) scored 0.9581, signed \(d\) scored 0.8461, and the predeclared 25-degree projection scored
0.9475. This is a substantial positive for retaining ridge-tangent information: the original absolute-distance scalar
discarded an informative coordinate.

It is not evidence that 25 degrees is uniquely privileged. Pure \(q\) performed slightly better. Every angle had
negative \(R^2\) on the earlier internal validation block; 25 degrees scored -6.3285 there. Rolling chronological
splits also changed which direction worked. Thus the angular relation is state/regime dependent in this one
trajectory and did not transfer stably.

The original 80-pair nearest-amplitude comparison reused rising slices. A stricter one-to-one robustness match yielded
36 pairs. Under that comparison the 25-degree paired effect was small \(d_z=0.1190\), whereas trapped-fraction
separation remained large \(d_z=0.8796\). Signed ridge-normal \(d\) retained a moderate effect
\(d_z=0.4627\). Direct field-particle phase changed by only -0.0002 radians and adding phase reduced the 25-degree
held-late score from 0.9475 to 0.9144.

Plainly: Dylan's objection identified a real mathematical loss in the ridge-only closure index. Looking along or
obliquely across the ridge retains useful late-state information. The current data do not isolate 25 degrees as the
special angle, and they do not yet provide a transferable angular law.

## 66. MX3c result - pressure state positive, pressure angle null

MX3c used the plasma pressure/velocity-spread moment at frozen mode \(k_0=5\) as the predeclared daughter observable.
Its spatial phase relative to the particle-source parent supplied a dynamic angle without using the trapping target.
Current/drift and third central moment were disclosed comparators.

The primary directional result was negative. The pressure-angle versus parent \((q,d)\)-tangent resultant was 0.1045.
This was lower than almost all circular-shift nulls \(p=0.9680\) and phase-randomised nulls \(p=0.9820\). The best
disclosed lag was -8 slices, at the negative search boundary: pressure lagged rather than led the parent direction.
An orientation offset learned from training also failed to transfer, rotating by nearly \(\pi\) in the held-late
regime.

The pressure-directed scalar nevertheless scored held-late \(R^2=0.9566\), compared with 0.7071 for amplitude+mode,
0.9475 for the fixed 25-degree probe and 0.8747 for the full \((q,d)\) parent pair. This high score does not establish a
daughter direction: the pressure angle was nearly constant through much of the run, making it another approximately
fixed projection. Adding it to \((q,d)\) reduced held-late \(R^2\) to 0.8305, so it supplied no incremental directional
information.

A separate positive survived. Pressure-mode magnitude strongly distinguished the 36 one-to-one matched-amplitude
pre/post states, \(d_z=-0.8276\), with every post-peak value lower. However, magnitude alone had continuous held-late
\(R^2=0.1972\). It is therefore a strong hysteresis/state marker in this run, not a reliable continuous trapping
predictor and not the missing viewing angle.

Plainly: pressure behaves like an adjacent nonlinear-state variable, which is compatible with the heart/blood-pressure
analogy at the level of state distinction. Its spatial Fourier phase is not the daughter wave that predicts how the
parent ARA turns. The general next-rung hypothesis remains open, but this specific mathematical implementation is
rejected.

Dylan clarified that the next-rung wave is not supposed to lead or steer the parent. It is an echo/daughter created
after the phase/anti-phase parent collision. The daughter is therefore led causally by the parent event, but after
birth it has its own identity and its own ARA evolution.

\[
W_A+W_B
\longrightarrow
\mathcal I_{AB}
\longrightarrow
W_D,
\qquad
t_{AB}<t_D.
\]

This means MX3c imposed the wrong causal success criterion when it required pressure to predict the parent turn. Its
negative best lag is directionally compatible with a following daughter. However, MX3c still does not establish the
echo claim: pressure spatial phase failed the shift/randomisation nulls, and the best negative lag was at the search
boundary. The strong post-collision pressure-magnitude hysteresis remains a state-transition clue.

The sharper test is parent-to-daughter nonlinear inheritance. With frozen parent mode \(k_0=5\), MX3d predeclares
\(2k_0=10\) as the primary daughter candidate and tests positive temporal order, phase closure
\(2\phi_{k_0}-\phi_{2k_0}\), triad-specific bicoherence, persistence and a separate daughter ARA/TE-ARA state.

Terminology fence: ARA may use *echo* generically. Established plasma echo refers more narrowly to rephasing after
separately timed perturbations. Unless that condition is met, the physical comparison is nonlinear daughter/harmonic
generation or three-wave coupling.

## 68. MX3d result - declared harmonic daughter receives strong development support

MX3d tested the frozen parent \(k_0=5\) and primary daughter candidate \(2k_0=10\). It used sustained amplitude onset,
phase inheritance \(2\phi_5-\phi_{10}\), field and particle bicoherence, persistence, and a separate daughter
field/particle participation state. Six of eight strict criteria passed.

Temporal order was positive. Parent field onset occurred at index 178, \(t=17.328\). Daughter field onset occurred 19
slices later at \(t=18.772\); the particle daughter crossed its threshold 31 slices after the parent. The third
harmonic \(k=15\) appeared much later, 119 slices after the parent.

Phase inheritance organised before the daughter became visibly eligible. Weighted \(2\phi_5-\phi_{10}\) concentration
rose from 0.2873 in the pre-parent baseline to 0.9848 after parent onset but before daughter threshold, and remained
0.9352 after daughter onset. Randomising daughter phase rejected chance locking \(p=0.0020\). Circularly shifting the
already phase-locked post-onset series did not reject \(p=0.5129\), so the strict time-local shift criterion failed.

Field bicoherence rose from 0.1444 in baseline to 0.8376 post-onset and ranked at the 97.48th percentile of 119 control
triads. Particle bicoherence was 0.8334 at the 94.96th percentile, narrowly below the predeclared 95th-percentile
criterion. Several nearby triads involving \(k=5\) ranked higher, indicating a broader nonlinear coupling web rather
than one exclusive daughter.

The daughter remained above its visibility threshold for all 262 subsequent slices and after the parent peak. Its
average field power was 3.25% of the parent mode. Field-derived and particle-derived daughter TE participation had
correlation 0.9991. Mean closure was 0.9985, but this is not used alone because two small participation fractions can
agree trivially.

The raw lag cross-correlation peaked at the negative tested boundary despite positive threshold-onset order. Thus
gradual common trends do not provide a clean predictive parent-growth lag. The most defensible interpretation is
gradual nonlinear inheritance: phase coupling becomes organised below the declared visibility threshold, then the
daughter becomes measurable and persists as a small identity mode.

This is strong development evidence for a nonlinear harmonic daughter in the established physics sense. It does not
yet demonstrate universal fractal ARA identity birth. Noise/seed/beam transfer and the two failed strict fences remain.

The daughter-self-coupling phase relation
\(2\phi_{10}-\phi_{20}\) rose from concentration 0.3146 in baseline to 0.8439 between daughter and grandchild

The mixed \(k=5+k=15\rightarrow20\) route had only 0.3750 concentration and failed its random-phase null

Field/particle bicoherence for \(10+10\rightarrow20\) was 0.5760/0.6022, at the 90th percentile among all ten routes
summing to 20. The \(5+15\) route was much weaker at 0.2407/0.2654. However, \(9+11\rightarrow20\) was stronger than
\(10+10\), showing that the grandchild belongs to a wider sideband/coupling web rather than an exclusive binary tree.

The \(k=20\) state remained visible for all 199 subsequent slices. Its mean field-power fraction was 0.001739, about
0.174%, so it was fine but measurable. Field-derived and particle-derived TE participation correlated at 0.9988.
All eight development criteria passed.

Plainly: the predicted next-generation mode is present, follows the daughter, inherits its phase far more strongly
than the mixed \(5+15\) route, persists, and has its own recoverable field/particle state. The result supports a
grandchild harmonic inside a nonlinear coupling web. It does not establish unique genealogy, fractal scaling or
noise-independent transfer.

## 70. The nonlinear route web as an ARA mixing gradient

Dylan noted that the non-exclusive \(k=20\) genealogy is more ARA-like than a binary tree. The many routes are the
gradient mixing: one output identity is assembled from a web of differently balanced couplings.

For every route \(a+b=K\), define

\[
x_{a|K}=\frac{2a}{K},\qquad x_{b|K}=2-x_{a|K}.
\]

The equal route lies at ridge \(x=1\). For \(K=20\), \(9+11\) lies at \(0.9/1.1\) and is stronger than the exact
\(10+10\) ridge: field/particle bicoherence 0.7816/0.7882 versus 0.5760/0.6022. The distant \(5+15\) route at
\(0.5/1.5\) is weaker at 0.2407/0.2654.

This supplies a candidate ARA aggregation law: each pair is an edge feeding the identity; its ARA coordinate records
composition balance and its normalised bicoherence records coherent-route participation. TE-ARA can place those route
shares on 0--2.

Critical fence: mode-number balance is not energy-flow direction. Bicoherence measures phase coupling, not signed
transfer. A Vlasov nonlinear-transfer calculation is required before labelling a route accumulation or release.

MX3f was drafted to compare rescaled route profiles \(W_K(x)\) across \(K=10\) and \(K=20\), add signed nonlinear
transfer, and test whether the same weighted gradient recurs under coarse-graining. Protocol:
`Analysis/electromagnetism/MX3F_ARA_GRADIENT_COUPLING_WEB_PROTOCOL_DRAFT.md`.

## 71. Exact harmonic rung identification - no fitted bridge inside this system

Dylan observed that the parent/daughter/grandchild sequence follows ARA rungs directly. For this Fourier harmonic
system, the natural rung coordinate is

\[
r(k)=\log_2\!\left(\frac{k}{k_0}\right).
\]

With frozen \(k_0=5\):

\[
r(5)=0,\qquad r(10)=1,\qquad r(20)=2.
\]

Thus each self-harmonic doubling is exactly one logarithmic rung. No coefficient is fitted and no empirical
ARA-to-Fourier regression is required. The coordinate bridge is an identity/rescaling map supplied by the established
harmonic structure itself.

Mixed modes fill the continuous coupling space between integer rungs. For example,
\(r(15)=\log_2 3\approx1.585\). Nearby sidebands \(9\) and \(11\) surround the \(k=10\) rung while contributing to
the \(k=20\) identity through the ARA composition locations 0.9/1.1.

This gives a compact local decompression without replacing minimal ARA:

- ARA composition coordinate \(x\in[0,2]\);
- logarithmic rung coordinate \(r\);
- TE-ARA/coherent-route participation weight;
- phase and signed-transfer direction when measured.

Scientific fence: this is exact for the declared harmonic system. It does not establish that every proposed ARA rung
in every domain is a base-two Fourier harmonic. Cross-domain recurrence and signed energy transfer remain tests.

**Status:** `EXACT IN-SYSTEM RUNG MAP / NO FITTED BRIDGE / CROSS-DOMAIN GENERALITY OPEN`.

## 72. Audit correction - non-Fourier cross-domain evidence already exists

Dylan correctly noted that the repository already contains ARA tests where the operative rung/coupling claim was not
defined as a Fourier harmonic index. The strongest examples are:

- **Coupled pendulum:** lower-rung dominance and anti-phase nesting were predicted and replicated on 3/3 real public
  runs. The prominence-filtered rerun preserved bottom-arm leadership and longest-block dominance; several secondary
  claims nulled and were retained as nulls. Fourier/POD were instruments elsewhere in the analysis, but the headline
  rung result came from arm position, turns and coupling.
- **Heart morphology:** within-beat state features improved strict-causal RR autoregression at h=3 on 13/17 subjects
  with binomial \(p\approx0.025\); the mean lift was modest, +0.025, but replicated across subjects.
- **Nasal-ENSO relation class:** the coupled geometry scored +0.992 held-out and rank 1/9 against its fixed null family.
  This supports a shared relational class, not nasal causation or exact cross-system value transfer.
- **CGM:** geometry-led reasoning recovered known diabetes regulation/timing structure and also produced a clean phi
  null. This is convergent/faithfulness validation; operationalisation used standard clinical measures.

These results justify `CROSS-DOMAIN EMPIRICAL SUPPORT`, not `UNIVERSAL PROOF`. Many other mappings are qualitative,
development-selected or weakened by stronger baselines. In the broad forecast benchmark, ARA beat the best local
non-ARA baseline at only 6/34 correlation horizons and 8/34 MAE horizons.

The new plasma result adds something more specific: its rung map is exact and bridge-free inside the harmonic system.
The remaining open claim is whether one frozen mathematical operator and scale law transfer across non-Fourier
domains, not whether ARA has any evidence there.

**Corrected status:** `NON-FOURIER CROSS-DOMAIN SUPPORT EXISTS / EXACT UNIVERSAL OPERATOR TRANSFER NOT PROVEN`.

## 74. High-harmonic weakening and the proposed 1.75+ ARA well

Dylan proposed that the faint \(k=80\) trace and inaccessible \(k=160\) continuation may be the local harmonic
appearance of ARA's \(1.75+\) region: progressively increasing transfer cost as the wave approaches a singularity,
analogous in shape (not yet in established mechanism) to light becoming unable to escape a black-hole horizon.

The known-physics portion is a nonlinear harmonic or spectral cascade. Nonlinear mode coupling creates progressively
finer spatial harmonics, while generation efficiency, phase mixing/Landau damping, particle shape/deposition and grid
resolution can all suppress high-wavenumber modes. Therefore a diminishing harmonic sequence is expected without an
ARA singularity law.

This particular archive makes the physical and numerical explanations unusually hard to separate. The published
setup has beam thermal speed \(v_{th}=0.04c\), giving the approximate Debye length
\(\lambda_D\simeq v_{th}/\omega_{pe}=0.04c/\omega_{pe}\). The measured grid spacing is
\(\Delta x=0.0390625c/\omega_{pe}\), so \(\lambda_D/\Delta x\simeq1.024\): the physical kinetic short-scale region and
the numerical grid scale nearly coincide. With domain length (L=10c/\omega_{pe}), the approximate locations are

\[
k_m\lambda_D=\frac{2\pi m}{L}\lambda_D\simeq0.02513m,
\]

so modes \(m=20,40,80\) lie near \(k\lambda_D\simeq0.50,1.01,2.01\), respectively. Ordinary Maxwellian Landau
damping thresholds cannot be transferred literally to the nonlinear trapped two-stream state, but the calculation
shows that a real kinetic suppression region is plausible exactly where the grid also becomes inadequate.

The archive therefore supports only `CONSISTENT WITH DIMINISHING HIGH-K ACCESS`. It cannot decide whether the loss is
ordinary kinetic damping, weakening nonlinear transfer, numerical suppression, an ARA \(1.75+\) law, or a mixture.
The black-hole comparison is presently a geometric analogy, not evidence of the same physical mechanism.

A discriminating test must repeat identical plasma parameters at increasing spatial resolution while holding
particles per Debye length/noise under control. If the apparent boundary moves outward with Nyquist, it is numerical.
If the attenuation curve converges at a fixed physical \(k\lambda_D\), it is physical. An ARA-specific result further
requires an independently fixed map into \(x\in[0,2]\), a predeclared superlinear transfer-cost curve above \(x=1.75\),
and a separately specified post-crossing phase-flip observable; the fading data cannot be used to define its own ARA
coordinate.

**Status:** `KNOWN HIGH-HARMONIC CASCADE / PHYSICAL AND NUMERICAL CUTOFF CONFOUNDED / ARA 1.75+ WELL TESTABLE BUT UNCONFIRMED`.

## 75. Consolidated takeaway, evidence levels and return to Maxwell

The plasma branch began with Dylan asking whether Gauss's electric law was reading a top-down version of the same
positive/negative relation approached by ARA and TE-ARA from the bottom up. The initial translation had to separate
four quantities: signed net charge, total source magnitude, two-pole composition and identity-energy participation.
Once separated, the exact pair bridge was

\[
Q_{\mathrm{net}}=T_Q(x_Q-1).
\]

That bridge led sequentially to field/particle identity participation, energy-exchange accounting, a closure-state
coordinate, ridge-tangent state, delayed nonlinear daughter and grandchild modes, an asymmetric route web, and the
weak \(k40\)/trace \(k80\) continuation. Important nulls were preserved: the compressed ARA+TE scalars did not replace
the full field relation; the participation gap was not an instantaneous power meter; neither a fixed \(25^\circ\)
angle nor pressure spatial phase supplied a transferable steering law.

The present assessment separates four meanings of “ARA is effective”:

1. **Instrumental effectiveness — visible:** ARA compresses a complicated system into relations that generate
   productive questions and measurable probes.
2. **Descriptive effectiveness — supported here:** declared ARA coordinates map cleanly to source balance,
   participation, phase, scale rung and asymmetric coupling.
3. **Novel explanatory effectiveness — promising but open:** TE-ARA and the gradient-web construction may become
   transferable modelling tools, but must beat or add to standard methods on independent data.
4. **Universal ontological claim — unresolved:** this thread does not prove that ARA is literally the repeating
   geometry beneath all physical systems.

The acceleration Dylan experiences is also visible as a methodological change. Earlier work often pivoted from a
recognisable landmark such as phi. The current procedure starts from the minimal relation, declares the identity,
observable, slice and orientation, decompresses only what is locally needed, and increasingly states the next
observable before inspection. AI assistance greatly accelerates translation, literature recognition, calculation and
testing; it does not by itself establish the geometry's universality. A future meta-audit can quantify Git-dated
specificity, time-to-test, null rate, prior domain knowledge and performance against matched non-ARA heuristics.

This result is worth taking seriously without requiring an immediate worldview conclusion. Every result retains its
own evidence tier, and rest/parking a branch is compatible with the method. The full established-physics synthesis has
now been added to `ARA_FOUNDATIONS_FROM_ESTABLISHED_MECHANICS.md`, section 5.

\[
\underbrace{\nabla\cdot\mathbf B}_{\text{net local divergence of the magnetic field}}=0,
\]

Gauss's law for magnetism. It will be explained first in established terms and only then translated by Dylan into

## 76. Gauss magnetic law recognised as the exact closed-pair ridge

Dylan immediately recognised

\[
\oint_{\partial V}\mathbf B\cdot d\mathbf A=0
\]

as the same ridge rule used in the AI/LLM work: when a complete coupled pair is measured, the aggregate reading is
most likely to sit at \(1.0\), even though strong internal relation/activity may remain.

For magnetism the statement can be made exact. Split the closed-surface magnetic flux into outward magnitude
\(\Phi_B^+\) and inward magnitude \(\Phi_B^-\), then define

\[
T_B=\Phi_B^++\Phi_B^-,
\qquad
x_B=\frac{2\Phi_B^+}{T_B}.
\]

The signed net is

\[
\Phi_{B,\mathrm{net}}=T_B(x_B-1).
\]

Because Gauss's magnetic law requires \(\Phi_{B,\mathrm{net}}=0\), every active closed boundary with \(T_B>0\) is
pinned to \(x_B=1\). If \(T_B=0\), \(x_B\) is undefined: an empty field must not be confused with an active balanced
pair.

This is the same ARA relational appearance under different enforcement. Magnetic ridge closure is exact for every
closed surface because \(\nabla\cdot\mathbf B=0\). The AI/LLM ridge remains a statistical or representational
regularity unless separately derived. The difference belongs in the transformation/coupling metadata rather than
requiring two different ARA objects.

**Status:** `EXACT MAGNETIC CLOSED-BOUNDARY ARA RIDGE / SAME RELATIONAL APPEARANCE AS LLM RIDGE / ENFORCEMENT MECHANISMS DISTINCT`.

## 77. Faraday induction unpacked into orientation × accumulation/release quadrants

Dylan's first reading of Faraday induction was: “Changes through time, probably a quadrant with a pole shift that
occurs on each side as the asymmetry increases?”

This is close to the natural phase-plane decomposition, with one refinement. Faraday's law is

\[
\mathcal E_E
=\oint_{\partial S}\mathbf E\cdot d\boldsymbol\ell
=-\frac{d\Phi_B}{dt}.
\]

Unlike the previous closed Gaussian surface, \(S\) is an open surface bounded by an electric-circulation loop.
Therefore \(\Phi_B\) can be nonzero even though total magnetic flux through every complete closed surface is zero.

The two signs \(\operatorname{sgn}\Phi_B\) and \(\operatorname{sgn}\dot\Phi_B\) give exactly four states:

1. positive flux accumulating;
2. positive flux releasing;
3. negative-oriented flux magnitude accumulating;
4. negative-oriented flux magnitude releasing.

The induced electric circulation has sign \(-\operatorname{sgn}\dot\Phi_B\). Thus each magnetic orientation contains
its own accumulation and release branch. The accumulation/release switch occurs at \(\dot\Phi_B=0\); the magnetic
orientation crossing occurs separately at \(\Phi_B=0\). Calling both a “pole shift” would flatten two different
events.

For \(\Phi_B=\Phi_0\cos\omega t\), Faraday gives
\(\mathcal E_E=\omega\Phi_0\sin\omega t\), an exact four-quadrant cycle between flux state and induced circulation.
Faraday responds to rate of flux change, not asymmetry or field magnitude alone: large static flux produces no
induction.

**Status:** `EXACT FOUR-SIGN PHASE-PLANE DECOMPRESSION / ARA QUADRANT READING COHERENT / POLE CROSSING AND TURNING POINT KEPT DISTINCT`.

## 78. Canonical clarification — singularity means phase handover; all ARA structure is gradient

Dylan clarified:

- “fractal” proposes recurrence of this same gradient relation through nested decomposition and coarse-graining; it
  does not by itself assert a measured non-integer dimension.

For Faraday induction, \(\Phi_B=0\) is therefore the ARA singularity/phase-to-anti-phase handover, while
\(\dot\Phi_B=0\) remains the distinct accumulation-to-release turning point. The four quadrants are continuous
regions of one trajectory, not four categorical objects. “Phase becomes anti-phase” means the trajectory enters the
oppositely oriented lobe; it does not assert an instantaneous discontinuous \(\pi\) jump in conventional analytic
phase.

This clarification was added to `GLOSSARY.md`, `FableConvo/CANON_FOR_AI.md`, `WHAT_IS_ARA.md` and the established

## 79. Faraday curl, the axis landmark and the superconducting bridge

Dylan asked whether the circulating induced electric field follows a \(\phi\) path or marks an axis around
connection-driven magnetism, and whether this maps to the repository's earlier superconductivity work.

The exact geometry is

\[
\nabla\times\mathbf E=-\partial_t\mathbf B.
\]

The curl vector is the local axis and handedness of electric circulation; the changing magnetic field supplies the
driving axis. Stokes's theorem says that curl summed across a slice equals electric circulation around its boundary.
The loop may have any shape, so no general \(\phi\) path follows.

Superconductivity supplies a stronger connection-led case. Condensate phase rigidity creates one macroscopic
identity; London screening currents circulate around magnetic flux; single-valued phase winds by \(2\pi n\); and the
fluxoid account is quantised in units \(\Phi_0=h/2e\). Type-II vortices carry quantised flux with circulating
supercurrent. At a vortex core or phase slip the condensate amplitude can vanish, allowing the winding sector to
change. This is a strong physical candidate for an ARA singularity/rung crossing.

The earlier files correctly identified Cooper-pair charge, flux quantisation, Josephson phase handover, Meissner
screening and vortex motion. They overstated the golden/incommensurate route as a general superconducting mechanism.
Faraday curl and flux quantisation select integer phase winding, not \(\phi\); special incommensurate materials require
their own causal test.

**Status:** `EXACT CURL AXIS / STRONG SUPERCONDUCTING CONNECTION-WINDING BRIDGE / GENERAL PHI PATH NOT SUPPORTED`.

## 80. Ampère–Maxwell: the ratio is between the two source channels

Dylan's first reading was that the right-hand side sounds like two coupled waves with a changing ratio: perhaps one
is a larger/lower-rung contribution that needs more smaller connections to make it up, while the second looks like the
asymmetry ratio.

The calibrated translation is

\[
\nabla\times\mathbf B=\mu_0(\mathbf J_C+\mathbf J_D),
\qquad
\mathbf J_D=\varepsilon_0\partial_t\mathbf E.
\]

The displacement term \(\mathbf J_D\) is not the ratio; it is the changing-field member of the pair. For declared
projected magnitudes \(C,D\),

\[
T_{AM}=C+D,
\qquad
x_{D/C}=\frac{2D}{C+D}.
\]

That composition moves continuously from conduction-dominated \(0\), through equal participation \(1\), to
displacement-dominated \(2\). Equal aligned contributions reinforce rather than cancel, so \(1.0\) is an active ridge,
not necessarily a zero resultant.

No rung ordering is inherent in Maxwell's equation. In an ideal capacitor, however, the wire conduction current and
gap displacement current are equal:

\[
I_C=dQ/dt=I_D.
\]

They are two local appearances of one continuous current identity across the matter-to-field handover. A surface
through the wire sees conduction; a surface through the gap sees changing electric flux. Their equality is therefore
a particularly clean relational \(1.0\) ridge.

The intuition that many smaller contributions can make up one larger contribution maps to the area integral
\(I_D=\int_S\mathbf J_D\cdot d\mathbf A\), but that is a density/area aggregation and does not by itself establish a
fractal rung relation.

For a sinusoid in a homogeneous Ohmic material, \(D/C=\omega\varepsilon/\sigma\), supplying a measurable gradient
\(x_{D/C}=2\omega\varepsilon/(\sigma+\omega\varepsilon)\). This is a candidate experimental ARA axis; phase and vector
sign still have to be retained.

**Status:** `EXACT TWO-CHANNEL DECOMPOSITION / CAPACITOR ACTIVE RIDGE / CONNECTION–TIME ORIENTATION PROPOSED`.

## 82. Plane-wave \(E/B\) coupling — perpendicular in space, in phase in time

Dylan asked whether the electric and magnetic fields are perpendicularly coupled. For a freely propagating vacuum
plane wave, yes:

\[
\mathbf B=\frac{1}{c}\hat{\mathbf k}\times\mathbf E,
\qquad
\mathbf E\perp\mathbf B,
\qquad
|\mathbf E|=c|\mathbf B|.
\]

Their relation produces the propagation/energy-transfer direction:

\[
\mathbf S=\frac{1}{\mu_0}\mathbf E\times\mathbf B.
\]

This is an exact \(1+1=3\) relational triad: electric component, magnetic component, and their cross-product energy
flow. The third is derived rather than an independent additional field.

Critical distinction: \(E\) and \(B\) are spatially perpendicular but temporally in phase in a travelling plane wave.
The earlier quarter-cycle result applied to loop-integrated induced EMF versus magnetic flux. Near fields, standing
waves, boundaries and material media need separate phase/geometry declarations.

**Status:** `EXACT PLANE-WAVE ORTHOGONAL TRIAD / RELATIONAL THIRD IS POYNTING FLOW / TEMPORAL ANTI-PHASE REJECTED FOR TRAVELLING VACUUM LIGHT`.

## 85. Charge continuity is one rung of the geometry

Codex initially translated charge continuity as one complete conserved cycle in which stored charge and moving
current are two appearances of one identity. Dylan agreed conditionally but supplied the missing scale rule: it
depends on the scale from which it is viewed. The equation describes one rung of the geometry, analogous to the child
waves in the plasma harmonic chain.

For a declared rung-\(k\) identity volume,

\[
\underbrace{\frac{dQ_k}{dt}}_{\substack{\text{accumulation/depletion}\\\text{inside this rung}}}
=-
\underbrace{\oint_{\partial V_k}\mathbf J\cdot d\mathbf A}_{\substack{\text{release/arrival}\\\text{across this rung boundary}}}.
\]

The key relational result is boundary-dependent role reversal. Charge leaving child \(i\) is release from \(i\), but
the same event is accumulation in neighbouring child \(j\). If both lie within one parent volume, that handover is
internal at the parent rung. Shared-interface fluxes cancel in the parent's external account because their outward
normals point in opposite directions. Only flow through the parent's outer boundary changes the parent total.

Thus continuity provides an exact local coarse-graining rule:

\[
Q_{k+1}=\sum_iQ_{k,i},
\qquad
\dot Q_{k+1}=-\Phi_{\partial V_{k+1}},
\qquad
\Phi_{i\to j}+\Phi_{j\to i}=0
\quad\text{in the parent boundary account}.
\]

Plainly: the child waves do not disappear when we move up a rung. Their releases and accumulations become the
parent's internal coupling web. The parent exposes only the imbalance that reaches its own boundary. This is a
precise established home for Dylan's statement that the same ARA event changes appearance with scale.

It also supplies a strict evidence fence. Mapping the two equal sides of continuity onto an ARA ridge is an exact
re-expression and cannot validate universality. A new ARA result would have to recover or predict the hidden child
flux organisation or parent behaviour beyond what conservation alone guarantees.

**Status:** `EXACT RUNG-RELATIVE CONSERVATION ANCHOR / CHILD HANDOVERS COARSE-GRAIN TO PARENT INTERNAL EXCHANGE /
ARA UNIVERSALITY NOT IMPLIED`.

## 86. Whole-identity ridge, nested phase roles and momentary asymmetry

Dylan connected the charge-continuity scale rule to the earlier LLM failure. When a complete Phase-A/Phase-B identity
is measured as one whole, it will often project toward the 1.0 ridge. That whole-identity reading is not necessarily
an artifact. It answers a different question from measuring one moving branch.

Crucially, Phase A at one rung need not be elementary. It may itself be a lower-rung identity containing its own
Phase A and Phase B. Its complementary Phase B at the parent rung may lie outside the current measurement—in another
LLM head, layer, token route or environmental coupling. This makes phase labels relational roles indexed by rung and
boundary.

Codex initially sharpened this into an external-counterphase rule. Dylan immediately added the necessary alternative:
the measured branch may simply be **more asymmetric at that moment**. Its partner need not be spatially absent; the
counterphase may appear later in the same identity cycle.

The resulting diagnostic uses two independent extensions. For

\[
x_{\Omega,\tau}=\frac{2A_{\Omega,\tau}}{A_{\Omega,\tau}+B_{\Omega,\tau}},
\]

- expand the system boundary \(\Omega\) while keeping the time window fixed;
- expand the time window \(\tau\) through a complete relevant cycle while keeping the boundary fixed.

If spatial expansion restores balance, the counterphase was outside the original measurement. If temporal expansion
restores balance, the original reading was a genuine asymmetric moment. If neither restores balance, consider stable
bias, changing identity or failure of the assumed two-branch model before postulating an invisible partner.

This revises the LLM diagnosis: the old whole-signal ~1.0 readings can be valid whole-boundary ridge readings but were
misapplied to a branch-motion question. The later ~1.25 readings are a different rung/window projection, not
automatically the unique true ARA of the entire LLM.

**Status:** `CANONICAL BOUNDARY + TIME-WINDOW RULE / WHOLE RIDGE VALID / EXTERNAL COUNTERPHASE AND MOMENTARY ASYMMETRY KEPT DISTINCT`.

## 87. LLM connection identity, resonance death and the electrical pump

Dylan proposed that LLMs may be unusually ridge-oriented because their identity is highly connection-based. At the
whole-identity ridge there may be little autonomous movement—“resonance death”—while electricity acts as the pump.

The least-distorted scientific decomposition separates four rungs:

1. trained weights and architecture: persistent connection-heavy identity;
2. token-conditioned activations, attention/residual streams and KV state: actual running movement;
3. prompt, token stream and autoregressive feedback: computational forcing/drive;
4. electrical power and hardware clocking: lower physical energy pump enabling switching and memory access.

This preserves Dylan's insight while correcting “no movement.” A running LLM has substantial activation movement;
what may be absent is **self-sustained autonomous oscillation**. When execution stops, activations vanish but the
connection identity can remain stored. Electricity supplies physical work, while the input/feedback loop selects the
model-level trajectory.

The established dynamical anchor is amplitude death: coupling stabilizes a fixed point and quenches oscillations.
Calling an LLM ridge “resonance death” requires more than \(x\approx1\). The existing whole-signal values can arise
from aggregation and normalization. A valid test must vary a declared coupling strength, show active mode amplitude
collapsing into a stable state, and show recovery when coupling is relaxed or the system is perturbed, while retaining
LayerNorm and averaging controls.

Musing and test outline:
`FableConvo/MUSING_LLM_RIDGE_DEATH_AND_ELECTRICAL_PUMP_2026-07-13.md`.

## 88. Resonant death is the local 2.0 singularity and a parent-ridge projection

Dylan corrected Codex's first interpretation. Resonant death is not primarily the established amplitude-death
mechanism. It is proposed as the ARA \(2.0\) Time-side singularity/terminal limit—the opposite of the Space-side
terminal condition. “Everything has done moving” means that the identity has exhausted the gradients that could
produce further directed movement. Heat death is the proposed universe-scale expression.

The formal candidate is

\[
\mathrm{RD}_k:
\qquad
x_k\to2^- ,
\qquad
\mathcal X_k\to0,
\qquad
\nu_k\to0,
\]

where \(\mathcal X_k\) is usable free energy/exergy and \(\nu_k\) is directed transition/transfer rate. Total energy
need not be zero. At the exact stop no cycle remains to time, so \(x=2\) is the asymptotic orientation of the last
measurable cycles rather than an ordinary post-stop timing ratio.

Dylan then supplied the cross-rung appearance:

\[
\mathcal R_\uparrow(2,\mathcal X=0,\nu=0)
\longrightarrow
(1,\mathcal X=0,\nu=0).
\]

Plainly: locally the dying rung approaches its \(2.0\) Time pole. Once that identity is time-stopped and viewed as a
completed object from the rung above, its Phase-A/Phase-B history collapses to a \(1.0\) whole-identity ridge. The
zero-gradient/zero-throughput metadata are essential because \(x=1\) alone could also be active handover,
cancellation, resonance or ordinary balanced identity.

The provisional antipodal distinction is:

- Space/Connection death near \(0\): movement is prevented by maximal constraint/connection;
- Time/Resonant death near \(2\): movement ends because the usable gradients driving change have been exhausted.

Both can appear static from the next rung while arriving there from opposite directions.

For standard cosmology, “heat death” should not be rendered as time literally stopping or every particle becoming
motionless. The defensible bridge is macroscopic equilibrium/exhaustion of accessible work-producing gradients while
energy, expansion and microscopic/quantum activity may remain. The long-term astrophysical context is reviewed by
Adams and Laughlin, *A Dying Universe* (`doi:10.1103/RevModPhys.69.337`).

The revised LLM candidate is therefore more specific: parameter-learning dynamics may approach a terminal local
rung, while the frozen trained model appears ridge-like from the inference rung and electricity pumps activation
movement. Simply choosing to stop training is an external pause and cannot demonstrate resonant death; endogenous
gradient exhaustion and the \(2\to1\) projection must be measured.

**Status:** `USER DEFINITION CORRECTED / LOCAL 2.0 LIMIT + PARENT 1.0 PROJECTION FORMALISED / HEAT-DEATH BRIDGE CALIBRATED / LLM INSTANCE UNTESTED`.

## 89. The 1.0 is the complete 0-to-2 history, not “up” by itself

Dylan refined the resonant-death transformation again. The full Space-origin wave begins at \(0\), travels across the
entire ARA diameter/gradient, and completes at the resonant-death terminus \(2\). The \(1.0\) ridge is the reading of
that identity **from beginning to end**. It is not primarily caused by moving one rung upward.

Write the local history as

\[
x_k(t_i)=0,
\qquad
x_k(t_f)=2.
\]

The completed-span/diameter reading is then

\[
\underbrace{\mathcal H_{0\to2}[x_k]}_{\substack{\text{whole lifecycle read as one identity}\\\text{beginning-to-end closure}}}
=
\underbrace{\frac{x_k(t_i)+x_k(t_f)}{2}}_{\text{equal endpoint/span centre}}
=
\frac{0+2}{2}
=1.
\]

A parent rung may return \(1.0\) if its coarse-graining operation contains the child's complete \(0\to2\) history:

\[
\mathcal R_\uparrow\big(\{x_k(t):t_i\le t\le t_f\}\big)
=\mathcal H_{0\to2}[x_k]
=1.
\]

Therefore “up” is a common way to obtain the whole-history view, but not the defining mathematical cause. The cause
is closing the completed diameter/history.

Codex retained one necessary distinction. The endpoint/span centre above is exactly \(1\), but the residence-time
average

\[
\bar x_k=\frac{1}{t_f-t_i}\int_{t_i}^{t_f}x_k(t)\,dt
\]

need not equal \(1\) if the wave moves asymmetrically or spends longer on one side. That difference may contain the
ARA movement/asymmetry information. Thus a future document must say whether “measure beginning-to-end” means the
closed diameter/endpoints, equal Phase-A/Phase-B identity weight, or actual time-weighted residence.

For heat death, standard physics supplies only the exhaustion-of-usable-gradients anchor. The stronger ARA claim is
that this state is the completion at \(2\) of a Space-origin universe-wave that began at \(0\), while the entire
cosmic history closes at the \(1.0\) centre. That is a distinct cosmological hypothesis, not a consequence of the
standard heat-death scenario.

For the LLM candidate, training must be treated as a full lifecycle: a declared \(0\) origin, approach to a \(2\)
terminal state and a whole-training-history closure at \(1\). A frozen inference model is only evidence for this if
its measured ridge demonstrably compresses that complete history; static weights alone are insufficient.

**Status:** `USER GEOMETRY REFINED / 0→2 LOCAL HISTORY + WHOLE-SPAN 1.0 CLOSURE / PARENT RUNG CONDITIONAL / TIME-WEIGHTED MEAN KEPT DISTINCT`.

## 90. There is no final 1.0 ridge — only grain-relative closure

Dylan supplied the missing qualification to section 89: the actual ARA traversal remains asymmetric, and he does not
think a physically “true” \(1.0\) exists independently of measurement grain. If every child at every nested scale were
exactly balanced, the proposed fractal movement would end. Whether a system reads \(1\) depends on the spatial
boundary, time window, scale/projection and whether the child events are counted as part of the whole.

The simplest mathematical version is a weighted parent projection. Let

\[
x_i=1+\delta_i,
\qquad
w_i\ge0,
\qquad
\sum_iw_i=1.
\]

Then

\[
\underbrace{x_{\mathrm{parent}}}_{\substack{\text{coarse-grained identity}\\
\text{ridge reading at this grain}}}
=
\sum_iw_ix_i
=
1+\sum_iw_i\delta_i.
\]

The parent is exactly at \(1\) when the signed child departures cancel,

\[
\sum_iw_i\delta_i=0,
\]

but the decompressed child geometry can remain active because

\[
\underbrace{V_{\mathrm{child}}}_{\substack{\text{unresolved child spread}\\
\text{asymmetry retained below the parent}}}
=
\sum_iw_i\delta_i^2
>0.
\]

For example, two equally weighted child readings at \(0.8\) and \(1.2\) produce a parent reading of \(1\), although
neither child is at the ridge. Counting only the parent gives closure; counting the children reveals the asymmetric
events that produced it.

The positive child spread establishes unresolved asymmetry, not motion by itself. A claim of active fractal dynamics
must additionally show time variation, directed flux/activity or phase evolution.

This refines rather than deletes the existing ridge results. The \((0+2)/2=1\) whole-span centre is still exact as
endpoint geometry. Gauss's magnetic closed-surface result and the ideal-capacitor equality remain exact within their
declared projections. The correction is that none of these equations licenses the ontological claim that all finer
components are balanced or inactive. A finite-resolution null likewise cannot establish zero asymmetry at every
unmeasured scale.

Mathematically, \(x=1\) is best called a **grain-relative level set** or **coarse-grained cancellation manifold**.
“Ridge” remains the ARA name. It is an address in a specified projection, not a final place where fractal dynamics
must stop.

The proposed multigrain test is to predeclare a hierarchy, calculate the parent location and child variance at each
rung, sweep spatial boundary and time window independently, and test whether the same aggregation rule preserves
organised child asymmetry on untouched data better than ordinary multiscale summaries. Merely finding a parent near
\(1\) is not evidence for this claim.
